```
## Delivery plan (experience-first)

Use this section to ship Review Studio as vertical slices that correspond to actual user-visible surfaces.

This is the recommended delivery model:

1. ship one foundation slice first
2. then ship page-by-page vertical slices
3. then run one hardening pass across the full loop

This keeps engineering work aligned with the actual product experience rather than marking technical subsystems complete while the UI loop is still broken.

### Slice 0: Foundation

#### Scope

- canonical CLI taxonomy
- detached Studio runtime startup/reuse
- worker contract support for `policyMode` and `reviewBasis`
- replay/resume scaffolding

#### Entry criteria

- locked Studio docs are accepted
- command taxonomy is frozen
- policy/default semantics are frozen

#### Definition of done

1. `nimbus review studio` launches or reuses Studio.
2. `nimbus review create --policy-mode none|auto|review` works.
3. `nimbus review open` is compatibility-only.
4. Studio runtime metadata exists and can be reused after relaunch.
5. Worker accepts canonical `policyMode` and `reviewBasis`.
6. Browser refresh and Studio restart can reconnect to active reviews from worker truth plus replay.

#### Exit artifact

- Studio can boot reliably enough for page work to proceed without reworking process lifecycle later.

### Slice 1: Home

#### Scope

- current branch context
- `New Review`
- `Resume active review`
- recent reviews
- branch switch banner

#### Definition of done

1. Opening Studio lands on Home.
2. Current branch context is visible and accurate.
3. `New Review` is prominent and reachable in one click.
4. `Resume active review` appears only when applicable and routes correctly.
5. Recent reviews render for the current branch.
6. Branch change banner appears without silently switching context.
7. Empty states are clear and actionable.

#### Tests / checks

- UI test for Home render states
- manual branch-switch scenario
- resume-active-review scenario

### Slice 2: New Review slide-over

#### Scope

- checkpoint resolution
- policy mode picker
- preflight summary
- review-start CTA

#### Definition of done

1. New Review opens without route change.
2. Default target resolves latest checkpoint from current branch using the locked fallback behavior.
3. Studio defaults policy mode from repo preference, falling back to `auto` on first run.
4. Policy mode selection is persisted to `.nimbus/studio.json`.
5. Preflight summary shows branch, checkpoint, policy mode, and readiness.
6. Hard-fail no-checkpoint state is explicit and understandable.
7. `Start Review` creates the correct review flow and routes into the Review Run page.

#### Tests / checks
```

```
function statusFromReviewEventType(eventType: string): ReviewResponse['status'] | null {
  switch (eventType) {
    case 'review_created':
    case 'review_enqueued':
      return 'queued';
    case 'review_succeeded':
      return 'succeeded';
    case 'review_failed':
      return 'failed';
    case 'review_cancelled':
      return 'cancelled';
    case 'review_policy_approved':
      return 'policy_approved';
    default:
      return null;
  }
}

function eventToLogEntry(eventType: string, data: Record<string, unknown>): ActivityLogEntry | null {
  if (eventType === 'heartbeat' || eventType === 'snapshot' || eventType === 'terminal') {
    return null;
  }
  const label = EVENT_LABELS[eventType] ?? eventType.replace(/_/g, ' ');
  let detail: string | undefined;

  if (eventType === 'review_analysis_provider_request_started' && typeof data.step === 'number') {
    detail = `Step ${data.step}`;
  } else if (eventType === 'review_analysis_model_output_received' && typeof data.step === 'number') {
    detail = data.repairAttempted ? `Step ${data.step} (repair)` : `Step ${data.step}`;
  } else if (eventType === 'review_finding_emitted') {
    detail = typeof data.description === 'string' ? data.description : undefined;
  } else if (eventType === 'review_context_changed_files_collected' && typeof data.changedFileCount === 'number') {
    detail = `${data.changedFileCount} files`;
  } else if (eventType === 'review_analysis_agent_started' && typeof data.model === 'string') {
    detail = data.model;
  } else if (eventType === 'review_context_budget_checked') {
    if (typeof data.estimatedTokens === 'number' && typeof data.tokenBudget === 'number') {
      detail = `${data.estimatedTokens.toLocaleString()} / ${data.tokenBudget.toLocaleString()} tokens`;
    }
  } else if (eventType === 'review_failed' && typeof data.message === 'string') {
    detail = data.message;
  } else if (eventType === 'review_succeeded' && typeof data.findingCount === 'number') {
    detail = `${data.findingCount} finding${data.findingCount === 1 ? '' : 's'}`;
  }

  return {
    id: `${Date.now()}-${Math.random().toString(36).slice(2, 8)}`,
    timestamp: Date.now(),
    label,
    detail,
  };
}

interface TimelinePhase {
  key: string;

}

interface ReviewedFilesSection {

}

function severityColor(severity: ReviewFinding['severity']): string {
  switch (severity) {
    case 'critical':
      return 'text-red-700 bg-red-50 border-red-200';
    case 'high':
      return 'text-orange-700 bg-orange-50 border-orange-200';
    case 'medium':
      return 'text-amber-700 bg-amber-50 border-amber-200';
    case 'low':
      return 'text-sky-700 bg-sky-50 border-sky-200';
    default:
      return 'text-slate-600 bg-slate-50 border-slate-200';
  }
}

function severityBarColor(severity: Exclude<ReviewSeverity, 'info'>): string {
  if (severity === 'critical') {
    return 'bg-red-500';
  }
  if (severity === 'high') {
    return 'bg-orange-500';
  }
  if (severity === 'medium') {
    return 'bg-amber-500';
  }
  return 'bg-sky-500';
}

function recommendationTone(recommendation: string | undefined): {
  containerClass: string;
  labelClass: string;
} {
  if (recommendation === 'approve') {
    return {

      labelClass: 'text-emerald-900',
    };
  }
  if (recommendation === 'comment') {
    return {
      containerClass: 'border-amber-200 bg-amber-50/50',
      labelClass: 'text-amber-900',
    };
  }
  if (recommendation === 'request_changes') {
    return {
      containerClass: 'border-red-200 bg-red-50/50',
      labelClass: 'text-red-900',
    };
  }
  return {
    containerClass: 'border-border bg-card/70',
    labelClass: 'text-foreground',
  };
}

function phaseStateClass(state: TimelinePhaseState): string {
  if (state === 'completed') {
    return 'border-emerald-200 bg-emerald-50 text-emerald-800';
```

```
export function ReportPage(): JSX.Element {
  const { reviewId, repo, branch } = useParams<{ reviewId: string; repo: string; branch: string }>();
  const hasBranchContext = Boolean(repo && branch);

  const [state, setState] = useState<LoadState>('loading');
  const [review, setReview] = useState<ReviewResponse | null>(null);
  const [errorMessage, setErrorMessage] = useState('');
  const [toastMessage, setToastMessage] = useState<string | null>(null);
  const [recoveringReview, setRecoveringReview] = useState(false);
  const [recoveryError, setRecoveryError] = useState<string | null>(null);
  const [refreshCycle, setRefreshCycle] = useState(0);
  const [activityLog, setActivityLog] = useState<ActivityLogEntry[]>([]);
  const [showReviewedFiles, setShowReviewedFiles] = useState(false);
  const seenEventIds = useRef(new Set<string>());
  const fallbackEventOrdinal = useRef(0);

  const isLive = review ? LIVE_STREAM_STATUSES.has(review.status) : false;

  useEffect(() => {
    seenEventIds.current.clear();
    fallbackEventOrdinal.current = 0;
    setActivityLog([]);
    setShowReviewedFiles(false);
    setRecoveryError(null);
    setRecoveringReview(false);
  }, [reviewId]);

  useEffect(() => {
    if (!reviewId) {
      setState('error');
      setErrorMessage('Missing review id in URL.');
      return;
    }

    let cancelled = false;
    const backgroundRefresh = state === 'loaded' && refreshCycle > 0;
    if (!backgroundRefresh) {
      setState('loading');
    }

    fetch(`${API_BASE}/api/reviews/${encodeURIComponent(reviewId)}`)
      .then(async (response) => {
        if (!response.ok) {
          const body = (await response.json().catch(() => null)) as { error?: string } | null;
          throw new Error(body?.error ?? `Request failed (${response.status})`);
        }

        const data = parseGetReviewResponse((await response.json()) as GetReviewResponse);

        if (!cancelled) {
          setReview(data.review);
          setState('loaded');
          setErrorMessage('');
        }
      })
      .catch((error) => {
        if (!cancelled) {
          setState('error');
          setErrorMessage(error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error));
        }
      });

    return () => {
      cancelled = true;
    };
```

Implemented and committed as `69a2308` with message: `feat(review-studio): finish slice 3 unified pre-run policy states`.

**What Changed**
- Unified policy + run rendering onto one surface in [ReportPage.tsx](/Users/nickdejesus/Code/nimbus/packages/report-ui/src/components/ReportPage.tsx):
  - Added explicit `policy_pending`, `policy_ready`, `policy_approved` in-route panels.
  - Added in-page policy editor + `Approve policy` action (POST `/api/reviews/:id/policy/approve`) without navigation.
  - Added explicit abandonment copy (`Return to Home`) clarifying no duplicate run is created.
- Removed dedicated policy-page behavior; [PolicyPage.tsx](/Users/nickdejesus/Code/nimbus/packages/report-ui/src/components/PolicyPage.tsx) is now a legacy alias wrapper to `ReportPage`.
- Route unification in [App.tsx](/Users/nickdejesus/Code/nimbus/packages/report-ui/src/App.tsx): `/policy/:id` and branch policy paths now render `ReportPage`.
- Updated history/branch route selection to always use `/reports/:id` in:
  - [ReviewHistoryPage.tsx](/Users/nickdejesus/Code/nimbus/packages/report-ui/src/components/ReviewHistoryPage.tsx)
  - [BranchReviewsPage.tsx](/Users/nickdejesus/Code/nimbus/packages/report-ui/src/components/BranchReviewsPage.tsx)
- Updated Studio/CLI route handoff to open Review Run route even for `policy_ready`:
  - [studio-create.ts](/Users/nickdejesus/Code/nimbus/packages/cli/src/app/reviews/studio-create.ts)
  - [create-from-deployment.ts](/Users/nickdejesus/Code/nimbus/packages/cli/src/app/reviews/create-from-deployment.ts)
  - [create-from-commit.ts](/Users/nickdejesus/Code/nimbus/packages/cli/src/app/reviews/create-from-commit.ts)

**Slice 3 Criteria Status**
- Distinct policy states visible on the Review Run route: satisfied.
- `policy_pending` progress without route swap: satisfied.
- `policy_ready` review/edit/approve on same route: satisfied.
- `policy_approved` handoff to queued/running on same route: satisfied.
- Refresh/restart policy-stage recovery from worker truth: satisfied (policy states load on `/reports/:id`; legacy `/policy/:id` still resolves to same surface).
- Explicit abandonment without hidden duplicate runs: satisfied via explicit in-page messaging and no implicit new-run creation.

> AGENT

The updated policy approval client logic assumes the wrong response shape and treats successful approvals as errors. This breaks the primary policy-approval UX and should be fixed before considering the patch correct.

Review comment:

- [P1] Handle policy approval 202 payload without parsing review — /Users/nickdejesus/Code/nimbus/packages/report-ui/src/components/ReportPage.tsx:1092-1097
  The policy approval endpoint returns an accepted payload (`{ reviewId, approvedPolicySha256 }`) rather than a full `GetReviewResponse`, so calling `parseGetReviewResponse(body)` on a successful 202 response throws `No review payload in response.`. In the policy-ready flow this surfaces as a false failure message after a successful approval and leaves the UI in an incorrect/error state until later polling/events recover it.

I’ll patch the policy approval handler to accept the worker’s `202` accepted payload shape, avoid parsing it as a full review snapshot, and rely on refresh/event updates for state transition. Then I’ll run the relevant `report-ui` tests.

> AGENT

Fixed.

I updated `handleApprovePolicy` in [ReportPage.tsx](/Users/nickdejesus/Code/nimbus/packages/report-ui/src/components/ReportPage.tsx) to support both success shapes:
- accepted payload (`{ reviewId, approvedPolicySha256 }`) from `202`
- full `{ review }` snapshot if returned

So it no longer calls `parseGetReviewResponse` unconditionally, and no longer shows a false failure on successful approval. For accepted payloads, it now updates local state to `policy_approved` and then refreshes.

I also updated the test in [ReportPage.test.tsx](/Users/nickdejesus/Code/nimbus/packages/report-ui/src/components/ReportPage.test.tsx) to cover the accepted-202 response path.  
Ran: `pnpm --dir packages/report-ui test -- src/components/ReportPage.test.tsx` (pass).  
Committed as `7619852` with message: `fix(report-ui): handle accepted policy approval response shape`.

  <results>
  The unified policy flow can stall in `policy_pending` and block policy approval visibility for active EventSource sessions because the page no longer polls review status during derivation. This is a functional regression in the new routing/UX path.

Review comment:

- [P1] Poll review status while policy derivation is pending — /Users/nickdejesus/Code/nimbus/packages/report-ui/src/components/ReportPage.tsx:847-854
  Now that policy-mode reviews are rendered on `ReportPage`, the `policy_pending` effect only updates the animation timer and never refreshes the review payload. In environments where `EventSource` is available, this can leave the UI stuck on “Preparing policy draft” because `review_policy_derivation_completed` events don’t map to a status transition here and heartbeat status updates are ignored, so users may never see `policy_ready` without a manual reload or stream error. Add periodic refresh during `policy_pending` (or explicitly transition status from policy derivation events).
  </results>

> AGENT

The unified policy flow can stall in `policy_pending` and block policy approval visibility for active EventSource sessions because the page no longer polls review status during derivation. This is a functional regression in the new routing/UX path.

Review comment:

- [P1] Poll review status while policy derivation is pending — /Users/nickdejesus/Code/nimbus/packages/report-ui/src/components/ReportPage.tsx:847-854
  Now that policy-mode reviews are rendered on `ReportPage`, the `policy_pending` effect only updates the animation timer and never refreshes the review payload. In environments where `EventSource` is available, this can leave the UI stuck on “Preparing policy draft” because `review_policy_derivation_completed` events don’t map to a status transition here and heartbeat status updates are ignored, so users may never see `policy_ready` without a manual reload or stream error. Add periodic refresh during `policy_pending` (or explicitly transition status from policy derivation events).

> DEVELOPER

how would you fix this?

> AGENT

I’d fix it with a small, explicit `policy_pending` refresh loop in `ReportPage` and keep the event path as a secondary accelerator.

1. In [ReportPage.tsx](/Users/nickdejesus/Code/nimbus/packages/report-ui/src/components/ReportPage.tsx), add a `useEffect` that runs only when `review?.status === 'policy_pending'` and schedules `setRefreshCycle(v => v + 1)` every ~2s (with cleanup).
2. Keep the existing derivation animation timer, but decouple it from status freshness.
3. Optionally harden SSE handling by mapping `review_policy_derivation_completed` to `policy_ready` in `statusFromReviewEventType` so transition is immediate when that event arrives.
4. Add a test in [ReportPage.test.tsx](/Users/nickdejesus/Code/nimbus/packages/report-ui/src/components/ReportPage.test.tsx): first fetch returns `policy_pending`, next poll returns `policy_ready`, assert the policy editor appears without manual reload.

That keeps Slice 3 behavior correct even when heartbeat/status events don’t advance local state.

> DEVELOPER

sounds good do it

> AGENT

I’ll implement both safeguards: periodic snapshot polling during `policy_pending` and event-to-status mapping for `review_policy_derivation_completed`, then add a regression test for pending→ready transition without manual reload.

> AGENT

Implemented.

I fixed [ReportPage.tsx](/Users/nickdejesus/Code/nimbus/packages/report-ui/src/components/ReportPage.tsx) in two ways:
- Added periodic snapshot refresh while `review.status === 'policy_pending'` (2s timer via `setRefreshCycle`) so the UI can naturally transition to `policy_ready` even if SSE status doesn’t advance.
- Added event-status mapping for `review_policy_derivation_started` → `policy_pending` and `review_policy_derivation_completed` → `policy_ready`.

I also added a regression test in [ReportPage.test.tsx](/Users/nickdejesus/Code/nimbus/packages/report-ui/src/components/ReportPage.test.tsx):
- `polls review status while policy derivation is pending` verifies pending → ready happens without manual reload.

Validation:
- `pnpm --dir packages/report-ui test -- src/components/ReportPage.test.tsx` (pass)
- `pnpm --dir packages/report-ui test -- src/components/ReportPage.test.tsx src/components/ReviewHistoryPage.test.tsx src/lib/review.test.ts` (pass)

> DEVELOPER

This was a bug found by nimbus. Verify and fix: F-002 · 🟡 medium · logic: Policy derivation poller can be left running after status changes away from policy_pending, causing extra GET /api/reviews requests during later review phases.
Fix: Tighten the dependency array and guards for the policy_pending polling effect so it only depends on stable identifiers and status, not on the refreshCycle it mutates. For example:

useEffect(() => {
  if (!review || review.status !== 'policy_pending') {
    return;
  }
  const timer = window.setTimeout(() => {
    setRefreshCycle((value) => value + 1);
  }, 2000);
  return () => {
    window.clearTimeout(timer);
  };
}, [review?.id, review?.status]);
This ensures that when the review transitions to policy_ready/queued/running, the effect is torn down and no new timers are scheduled. Also consider the same pattern for the policyProgressCycle effect (depending only on status and maybe reviewId).

packages/report-ui/src/components/ReportPage.tsx L134–170

> AGENT

Fixed and verified.

I updated [ReportPage.tsx](/Users/nickdejesus/Code/nimbus/packages/report-ui/src/components/ReportPage.tsx) so both policy-pending timers are status-scoped and no longer depend on the state they mutate:

- `policyProgressCycle` timer now uses `setInterval` with deps `[review?.id, review?.status]`
- `refreshCycle` poll timer now uses `setInterval` with deps `[review?.id, review?.status]`
- Both clean up with `clearInterval`, so when status leaves `policy_pending`, timers are torn down and do not keep scheduling.

I also kept the derivation event mapping in place (`review_policy_derivation_started/completed`) so status can advance promptly from stream events.

Validation:
- `pnpm --dir packages/report-ui test -- src/components/ReportPage.test.tsx`
- `pnpm --dir packages/report-ui test -- src/components/ReportPage.test.tsx src/components/ReviewHistoryPage.test.tsx src/lib/review.test.ts`

Committed as `5087f9a`:
`fix(report-ui): scope policy-pending timers to status lifecycle`

> DEVELOPER

New bugs from nimbus. Verify fix, push: F-003 · 🟡 medium · logic: Policy approval handler does not guard against a stale approvedPolicySha256 echo in the 202-accepted flow, allowing local state to overwrite a newer server-approved policy hash.
Fix: Before applying the optimistic local approvedPolicy + approvedPolicySha256 path, verify that the 202 response body’s reviewId matches the current reviewId and that no newer review payload has been loaded. Also, when a follow-up GET /api/reviews/:id completes, prefer the server’s approvedPolicySha256 over any optimistic local value. For example, include the current review?.updatedAt or a local version counter in state and only apply the optimistic update if it still matches; otherwise, discard it and rely on the next poll result.

packages/report-ui/src/components/ReportPage.tsx L838–905
F-004 · 🔵 low · logic: Policy derivation polling and progress interval timers are not cleared when the review transitions out of policy_pending, which can cause redundant polling and unnecessary state updates.
Fix: Update the useEffect dependencies and guards to clear intervals as soon as review.status leaves policy_pending. One simple approach is to depend on review?.status and, inside the effect, capture the current status in a local variable; if it changes before the next tick, bail early. Alternatively, track a policyDerivationActive boolean and include it in the dependency list, and when it becomes false, avoid scheduling further intervals.

> AGENT

I’ll verify both reports against the current `ReportPage` code, then patch stale-approval race handling plus timer teardown guards, run `report-ui` tests, and push the branch.

> AGENT

Verified and fixed both issues, then pushed to remote.

What I changed in [ReportPage.tsx](/Users/nickdejesus/Code/nimbus/packages/report-ui/src/components/ReportPage.tsx):
- Added an authoritative snapshot version guard for policy approval optimistic updates.
- Added `reviewId` validation for the 202 accepted body before applying optimistic `approvedPolicySha256`.
- Ensured authoritative server snapshots (GET/recover/fail/full review payload) are tracked and preferred over optimistic state.
- Added timer tick guards for `policy_pending` intervals so they no-op if status is no longer `policy_pending` (or review id changed), preventing redundant updates.

Test updates in [ReportPage.test.tsx](/Users/nickdejesus/Code/nimbus/packages/report-ui/src/components/ReportPage.test.tsx):
- Added regression coverage that mismatched accepted `reviewId` is ignored (no stale optimistic handoff/hash overwrite).

Validation run:
- `pnpm --dir packages/report-ui test -- src/components/ReportPage.test.tsx`
- `pnpm --dir packages/report-ui test -- src/components/ReportPage.test.tsx src/components/ReviewHistoryPage.test.tsx src/lib/review.test.ts`
- All passed.

```
### Slice 4: Review Run, active states

#### Scope

- `queued`
- `running`
- quiet progress
- `Agent Thinking`
- live event updates

#### Definition of done

1. The route stays stable while the review moves through `queued` and `running`.
2. Quiet progress is understandable at a glance.
3. `Agent Thinking` supports curated summaries and raw stream toggle.
4. Event replay/dedupe preserves ordering by `reviewId + seq`.
5. Browser refresh restores current state and continues live updates.
6. Studio restart reconnects and catches up correctly.
7. No synthetic terminal states are invented locally.

#### Tests / checks

- replay/dedupe coverage
- UI state merge coverage for snapshot + stream
- restart/refresh manual scenario

### Slice 5: Review Run, terminal states and fix loop

#### Scope

- `succeeded`
- `failed`
- `cancelled`
- `Fix with agent`
- `Review current environment`
- `Run another review`
- explicit export/apply

#### Definition of done

1. Success, failure, and cancelled states render distinctly.
2. Failure state prioritizes `Retry same inputs` and `Retry with policy review`.
3. `Run another review` starts a fresh checkpoint-based review flow.
4. `Fix with agent` creates or reuses mutable environment state without creating a new review run.
5. `Review current environment` creates a new validation review against current environment state.
6. Validation review does not silently downgrade to the original checkpoint snapshot.
7. Export/apply back to the local repo is explicit only.
8. Cleanup rules do not delete mutable environments with unexported edits.

#### Tests / checks

- terminal-state UI coverage
- remediation-loop coverage
- validation-review basis coverage
- explicit export/apply safety checks

### Slice 6: Hardening pass

#### Scope

- end-to-end polish
- recovery reliability
- cleanup safety
- compatibility cleanup

#### Definition of done

1. All page slices work together without terminal prompts mid-loop.
2. Full flow works:
   - Home
   - New Review
   - Policy or direct run
   - Active review
   - Terminal review
   - Fix with agent
   - Review current environment
   - Export/apply explicitly
3. Studio survives terminal exit, browser refresh, and local service restart.
4. Compatibility commands still work but are no longer the primary documented path.
5. QA checklist from the Studio build plan passes end-to-end.

## Experience-based release gate

Do not mark Review Studio “implemented” just because the APIs and command handlers exist.

Mark it implemented only when:

1. Slice 0 through Slice 6 are complete.
2. Each slice satisfies its own definition of done.
3. The end-to-end user loop works page-by-page without needing undocumented operator intervention.

```

> AGENT

Yep. From the plan:

**Slice 4: Review Run, active states**
- Scope: `queued`, `running`, quiet progress, `Agent Thinking`, live event updates.
- Done means: stable single route through queued/running, understandable quiet progress, `Agent Thinking` with curated + raw toggle, replay/dedupe by `reviewId + seq`, refresh/restart reconnection works, and no fake terminal states.
- Checks: replay/dedupe coverage, snapshot+stream state-merge coverage, restart/refresh scenario.

**Slice 5: Review Run, terminal states + fix loop**
- Scope: `succeeded`, `failed`, `cancelled`, plus `Fix with agent`, `Review current environment`, `Run another review`, explicit export/apply.
- Done means: distinct terminal UIs, failure prioritizes retry actions, `Run another review` creates fresh checkpoint-based run, `Fix with agent` reuses/creates mutable env without new review run, validation review targets current mutable env (not silently checkpoint), export/apply is explicit, cleanup won’t delete unexported mutable edits.
- Checks: terminal UI coverage, remediation-loop coverage, validation-basis coverage, explicit export/apply safety checks.

> DEVELOPER

Which slice is more difficult to implement? Rate each one on a scale of 1-10, 10 being the hardest/most complex

> AGENT

Slice 5 is harder.

- Slice 4: **7/10**
- Slice 5: **9/10**

Why: Slice 4 is mostly run-state UX + event-stream correctness/replay. Slice 5 adds new lifecycle actions and mutable-environment semantics (`Fix with agent`, validation reviews, explicit export/apply, cleanup safety), which is more cross-system and higher risk.
